([marcusgoll/deep-loop-plugin](https://github.com/marcusgoll/deep-loop-plugin/tree/HEAD/legacy/claude-code/skills/blind-critic) · ★1)
- **blind-curse-of-knowledge**: Use when writing docs, error messages, onboarding, tutorials, API designs, UX copy, or teaching — or when something you built seems self-explanatory. Applies the curse of knowledge (Camerer, Loewenstein & Weber 1989; Newton's tappers-and-listeners 1990): expertise makes you unable to simulate not-knowing. ([jpoindexter/blind-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/blind-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/blind-curse-of-knowledge) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **blind-db-debugging**: Diagnose database issues via SQL row counts without seeing actual data values. - **blind-design-review**: Use when asked whether a pull request's implementation is reasonable, what it should have done, or whether its approach holds up — "is this PR reasonable", "review the approach", "deep review", "blind review", "would we have built it this way" — rather than a line-level bug hunt over the diff. - **blind-designing**: Use when a change should be designed independently of any existing implementation — to get an unanchored yardstick for judging a PR, or several independent takes on how to build something from a written brief or ticket — "design this blind", "how would we build this from scratch", "what should this have looked like". - **blind-dunning-kruger**: Use when operating in an unfamiliar domain, when something "looks right" but hasn't been verified, when confidence is high without external checks, or when evaluating one's own competence at a task. Applies Kruger & Dunning (1999) — the skills needed to perform are the skills needed to judge performance. - **blind-env**: Keep secrets out of the agent's context: never read, print, search, copy, or edit environment and deployment config files — .env / .env.* / *.env / .envrc, config YAML (config*.yml, application*.yml, settings*.yaml, secrets*.yaml, values*.yaml), Helm values and charts, helmfiles, and kubeconfigs. - **blind-review**: Lance une relecture À L'AVEUGLE d'une partie du code par un agent au contexte neuf, qui n'a ni l'intention de l'auteur, ni les messages de commit, ni le plan — seulement les fichiers. Il reconstruit lui-même ce que le code est censé faire, puis cherche les écarts. À utiliser après avoir écrit une fonctionnalité, avant de la fusionner, ou quand on veut un avis que le raisonnement de l'auteur n'a pas déjà contaminé. Ne pas utiliser pour une relecture de style ou de design d'interface (voir le skill impeccable).
